French police arrested 1,000 people during protests about the cost of living and education, with that number expected to rise, according to the country’s interior minister Laurent Nunez
The government estimated that at least 400,000 people had marched across France. Trade unions estimated the number at double that.
Two serious injuries were sustained during the protests, Nunez said, including a 20-year-old man who had his fingers blown off.
A demonstration on the outskirts of Paris was largely peaceful but was marred by violence at the end, with police using a water cannon and teargas to quell the crowds.
More than 70,000 police were on the streets of French cities, with shops boarded up and many museums closed.
In Italy, nine were arrested and more than 370 people “briefly detained” by police.
Protests also took place in Belgium, Spain, Portugal and Albania over different issues.
A peaceful protest during the day in Paris descended into violent clashes with police by the time night fell.
The sound of pop music blaring and chanted slogans was swapped for the sound of teargas being released and fireworks being released, according to reports.
Eyewitnesses say the mood soured at the end of the march, where rows of police officers and vans awaited the crowd.
Protesters reportedly fired fireworks towards law enforcement officers, who responded with charges and baton strikes.
Students involved in a Paris demonstration questioned the police response Photograph: Hannah McKay/Reuters The AFP news agency quoted David, a protester who gave only his first name. He is said to have appeared shaken and had a gash on his face, which he said was caused by a police officer throwing a baton at him.
“The police started moving towards everyone, telling them to get down and back off. They were just taking people down, you know, beating protesters,” he said.
Mike, a 23-year-old student who also declined to give his surname, said: “Look around, they’re just teenagers. Is it normal for teenagers to be teargassed and beaten with batons?”
For Jessica, a 24-year-old student, said: “I just don’t understand why there’s so much violence. I feel like our right to protest is being trampled on.”
As night fell, police began clearing the streets and protesters started to disperse.
Italian police said nine people were arrested, and 371 briefly detained during protests in eight cities.
In a statement, the police said 110 of those either arrested or detained were minors.
My colleague Angelique Chrisafis has been reporting from the protest in Paris today, which has since dispersed.
She writes about how a largely peaceful demonstration on the outskirts of the French capital was marred at the end by violence.
The mood in Paris on Saturday afternoon was peaceful and lighthearted, with brass bands playing and children and grandchildren present. Some people carried banners critical of the government’s policing, saying: “Oppression breeds rebellion” and “The right to protest”.
But at the end of the Paris demonstration, at Porte de Vincennes on the city’s eastern edge, different groups of masked men dressed in black emerged who had not appeared to have taken part in the peaceful march. Some groups of masked men threw glass bottles and stones at police, who responded with teargas, water cannon and stun grenades. A bin was set alight and fireworks were set off towards police.
A man reportedly had his fingers blown off during trouble in France , a minister has said.
France’s interior minister – equivalent to the home secretary in the UK – Laurent Nunez said two people had been “seriously injured” during what he called “an extremely elevated level of violence” at today’s protests.
A 20-year-old man in Toulouse, in southern France, is said to have had his fingers blown off, and the other serious injury occurred in Clermont-Ferrand in the centre of the country. He did not give details of either case.
In an update, Nunez said “ultra-left” groups had thrown projectiles and aimed fireworks at police to provoke clashes.
Projectiles were thrown during Saturday’s clashes between protesters and police Photograph: Dylan Martinez/Reuters He struck a more positive tone than last week when violence erupted across French cities during protests about the level of investment into public services and the cost of living.
Nunez said that, while some shops and businesses had been vandalised and looted today, “the scale was far smaller” than a week earlier. He stressed that shopping centres had been well protected this time.
In Paris, where the main demonstration passed off largely peacefully but clashes broke out with police as the crowd dispersed, Nunez said “several thousand radical activists gathered at the rear of the march, including about 500 individuals identified as rioters”.
Ensuing clashes in the French capital lasted for around two hours, he said.
The minister said that there were first-time clashes in the southern cities of Cannes and Montpellier between far-right activists who sought confrontations with “ultra-left militants” who he said claimed to be there to protect businesses.
The authorities will remain “on high alert” through the night and into Sunday, he said.
Demonstrations were held in the Italian cities of Rome, Turin and elsewhere, with some turning violent.
As we reported earlier, security was heightened in Rome, where authorities locked down luxury shopping streets and stepped up controls around the Circus Maximus and Piazza del Popolo.
A small number of protesters tried to force their way into the Colosseum, but were ushered out by guards.
Protesters in Rome attempted to force themselves into the Colosseum Photograph: Vincenzo Livieri/Reuters Elsewhere in the city, several hundred students demonstrated peacefully to stress they were not rioters after unrest last week, particularly in Milan.
Riccardo Ponzio, national president of an Italian far-right student association, said: “I think the images from last week were truly something terrible, especially for the young people, for the students, for the vast majority of the younger generations who want to study … and do not accept being represented by those who use issues and needs to devastate entire cities.”
A separate anti-war march took place in central Rome. Organisers sought to distance themselves from potential unrest, urging participants to keep their faces uncovered and hands visible.
But it was these marchers who briefly clashed with police near the Piazza della Repubblica, even as organisers were trying to hold the crowd back using batons and teargas.
In the end, the protesters pulled back when a water cannon was turned on. They retreated to a location about 500m away.
French police arrested almost 1,000 people on Saturday during the protests held across the country, interior minister Laurent Nunez said.
He told reporters that the number “will rise” as more data from police becomes available.
Although marches over the cost of living “passed off well” in cities, officers found themselves later confronted with “an extremely elevated level of violence” from “ultra-left” groups, Nunez said.
France's interior minister Laurent Nunez has given an update on the number of arrests at Saturday’s protests Photograph: Julien de Rosa/EPA Jean-Luc Mélenchon , the radical-left presidential candidate who marched in the Paris rally today, said in a social media post that the French police “ruined the day” by “shooting indiscriminately” at Paris protesters.
“In Paris, as a way to ruin the day and blow off some steam, the so-called ‘forces of law and order’ are firing tear gas and shooting indiscriminately into the crowd at the tail end of the march, all to secure footage for domestic television channels, which have spent the entire week broadcasting the very images that incited the killer in Trappes,” he wrote. “A non-stop display of shame.”
In addition to the earlier footage of police using tear gas against protesters in Lyon, we are now seeing tear gas being deployed in the cities of Paris, Marseille, Nantes, and Bordeaux.
Though the protests have been relatively contained so far, the use of tear gas has become an increasingly common tactic for police to disperse crowds at the end of organized rallies, even without an immediate threat. And taking past demonstrations into consideration, these situations often become more volatile as the sun begins setting and the organized gatherings are ending.
French gendarmes stand in position amid tear gas and clashes during a demonstration organized by labour unions in Paris as part of nationwide protests in France, October 24, 2026. Photograph: Pedro Nunes/Reuters View image in fullscreen
A man runs amid tear gas during a demonstration in Lyon, France, October 24, 2026. Photograph: Lisi Niesner/Reuters View image in fullscreen
Masked protesters take cover behind a plywood board amid clashes and tear gas during a demonstration organized by labour unions in Paris, France, October 24, 2026. Photograph: Pedro Nunes/Reuters The Interior Ministry has put today’s nationwide turnout at 410,000 total protesters across France .
Clashes began to erupt late on Saturday, following a pattern of otherwise peaceful protests growing more tempestuous as the day draws to a close.
The Paris march began calmly, but near its endpoint at Porte de Vincennes, some protesters threw projectiles while police fired volleys of tear gas and used water cannons to disperse people.
Authorities had confined the capital’s march to the city’s southeastern edge, keeping protesters away from central neighborhoods hit by last weekend’s destruction.
Police in Paris say that about 110,000 people have marched across France’s capital today, but that the “situation remains calm” according to reporting from AFP. Authorities have now begun dispersing the crowds.
The 110,000 figure is roughly the same amount as the number of protesters in Paris last weekend. Considering the fears many people had that the protests could turn violent, as well as the rainy weather across the area, the number is an impressive showing.
The police also said more than 3,000 checks have been carried out and 65 people were arrested during the demonstrations in Paris.
Meanwhile in Albania , more than 2000 police have been deployed around Tirana on the 12th day of student-led protests amid reports of concerted efforts to stop demonstrators entering the capital.
Protests initially spurred by fury over a planned Trump family-backed resort in Albania have turned increasingly violent as students have joined what growing numbers are now calling a revolution to oust prime minister Edi Rama. Today, rally numbers swelled with demonstrators traveling to Tirana from cities across the Balkan state and as teachers, doctors and labour unions also weighed in.
But police also stand increasingly accused of heavy-handed tactics amid reports of civilians being plucked from the streets by masked officers and riot police increasingly resorting to using rubber bullets, batons, tear gas and water cannons to disperse crowds.
Speaking to the Guardian after spending several hours at Tirana’s National Trauma hospital earlier in the day, Kei Brisku, who heads the Abania Rehabilitation Centre for Trauma and Torture, said there were growing concerns about “the mass and random use” of rubber bullets.
“Nine protestors, five women and four men, were badly wounded by rubber bullets today and are now in hospital,” he said.
“The use of rubber bullets against violent protesters may be justified under applicable regulations ... however, their indiscriminate use against crowds is unacceptable, as it risks harming peaceful protesters and bystanders who pose no threat.”
In power for 13 years, Rama has resolutely refused to resign. While he has offered to discuss the “ legitimate concerns” of students, he has ruled out talking with hooligans who resort to violence.
Last week, the socialist leader told the Guardian the “violence in Tirana is very real,” as he dismissed accusations that his government was employing underhand tactics to fast-track the controversial resort championed by Jared Kushner, the US president’s son-in-law.
The NFL (American football league) closed its “NFL House” in Paris as the city braced for street protests with a heavy police presence and storefronts boarded up.
The league announced the closure to fans on Saturday morning through its “OnePass” app, according to the AP, citing “potential accessibility issues.”
Security concerns have overshadowed the NFL’s planned debut in Paris. The New Orleans Saints will play the Pittsburgh Steelers on Sunday at Stade de France in the league’s first-ever regular-season game in France.
Major museums including the Louvre and Orsay Museum are also closed Saturday, though tourists were seen lined up to enter Notre Dame Cathedral.
Former Pittsburgh Steelers player Donte Stallworth participates in the Hail Mary event on the banks of the Seine river in Paris, Friday, 23 October, 2026 in the build up to the Saints against Steelers NFL game. Photograph: Dave Shopland/AP View image in fullscreen
Thousands of pensioners and retirees protesting for pension protections in Madrid Photograph: Aaron Heredia/ZUMA Press Wire/Shutterstock Spanish authorities say about 25,000 demonstrators have marched through the country’s capital demanding protections to the state pension scheme.
Spain is voting in a snap parliamentary election in a few weeks and this has been a key topic of concern. Spain’s Socalist prime minister Pedro Sanchez, who called the snap election after struggling to get pension reforms through parliament, has accused conservative parties of holding back inflation-linked increases.
Protesters marched from the Atocha train station to the Gran Via shopping avenue in the Spanish capital.
Some expressed concern about portions of their state pensions being privatised in the future. About 6.8 million Spaniards receive a state pension and fiscally conservative, right-wing politicians have raised questions over how sustainable the current system is.
Spain has also seem major protests in recent months over increasingly unaffordable housing and renters’ rights.
Some are, namely in certain cities and low-income suburbs. Videos filmed by students and teachers have shown leaks, scurrying rats, holes in walls and ceilings, broken toilets and not enough chairs or tables, meaning teachers have to carry them from classroom to classroom. Some children said they had to learn in class while leaks were pouring into buckets, or move desks to avoid getting wet. Many school classrooms experienced temperatures over 35C (95F) in the heatwaves of last May and June.
Estimates in recent years have pointed to thousands of French schools at all levels, both primary and secondary, in need of repair. These include many 1960s school buildings. French regions are responsible for upkeep of high school buildings, but have asked for higher budgets from the government. A senate report has advised that billions need to be spent in the coming years to renovate and repair schools, as well as to address the impact of the climate crisis.
How dilapidated are French schools and how bad is inequality in the education system?
Tens of thousands of people have turned out to the heavily policed demonstrations across France and they have been, on the whole, quite contained so far.
Some sections of the crowd could be seen singing and dancing in Paris as they walked along the route, which was pushed to the city’s fringes at the last minute as police sought to move the march away from the fortified city centre.
In Lyon , police used teargas early on in the banned demonstration to disperse some of the crowd but people were still able to walk on.
In the last half hour there’s also a been of bit of rough activity in Marseille between police and a few dozen people. The French broadcaster BFMTV has shown live footage of masked attendees throwing bins, flares going off and spot fires in the street. There are cordons of riot police around.
Overall though, the heavy policing has appeared to be successful so far in staving off the type of looting, ransacking and violent activity we saw on the fringes of the marches last week. The government has added on 20% more officers, for a 70,000 strong force across the country.
Earlier today, the French interior minister had said protest numbers – at about 36,000 in the morning- were about half of what they were last Saturday.
At the Paris march, Nadège, a communications worker, said she was at Place de la République last Saturday when teargas hit. But that hadn’t deterred her.
I’m out again peacefully today to support everyone who is brave enough to demonstrate. It does take courage. People are afraid now.
We have to keep our rights alive. We’ve seen what happens in the US when a bad president is in power. In France, it’s our legal right to protest in the street and I’m here to defend that.
Photograph: Angelique Chrisafis/The Guardian The French president had hosted student representatives for a highly anticipated meeting yesterday afternoon.
Going in, the student leaders had already expressed cynicism over whether the French president would adequately address their concerns.
Pupils in Paris began protests over the quality of their education four weeks ago – and their spirit has since inspired demonstrations elsewhere, across the nation and also into other countries, such as Italy and Greece.
French high schoolers say they’re fed up with the deteriorating state of their school buildings, overworked and underpaid teachers, and packed classrooms.
They’re demanding school renovations and upgrades, better study conditions and the scrapping of the Parcoursup univeristy admissions platform which was introduced in 2018 and has long been criticised for lack of transparency.
Macron held a two-and-a-half hours-long meeting with 20 students.
Nathanael Combary, president of campaign group High School Renewal, said the president had been “attentive” and they would continue talks with Prime Minister Sébastien Lecornu on Monday on proposed changes like reform of Parcoursup.
But he and other student leaders called on their peers to continue protesting this weekend.
Student leader Nathanael Combary was one of 20 representatives who met with Emmanuel Macron at the Élysée Palace on Friday for negotiations. Photograph: Jeanne Accorsini/SIPA/Shutterstock The radical-left presidential candidate Jean-Luc Mélenchon, leader of the La France Insoumise party, is also marching in the Parisian procession.
Speaking to the press earlier he said the protests would continue every week until Emmanuel Macron and his government addressed the public’s demands.
He said Macron “is going to understand that this is going to repeat itself every week” and urged the president, “there is still time for him to concede” to the students’ wishes.
Macron met with student leaders on Friday for the first time since the demonstrations began a month ago. More on that meeting next.
French leftist La France Insoumise (LFI) leader Jean-Luc Melenchon (c) in the Paris protest on 24 October 2026. Photograph: Firas Abdullah/EPA

</details>

Fireworks and tear gas as protesters and riot police clash in Paris
Fireworks and tear gas as protesters and riot police clash in Paris Close
Police have clashed with protesters in Paris as some demonstrations in France turned violent on Saturday.
A largely peaceful day of protests turned into unrest after nightfall, as protesters were seen throwing objects and fireworks at police.
Around 70,000 officers were deployed across France for a second consecutive weekend and used tear gas and water cannons to disperse crowds.
The Interior Ministry estimated around 400,000 people took part in Saturday&#x27;s demonstrations, with nearly 1,000 arrests made.
Europe correspondent Nick Beake reports from Paris, where he says there are few signs the unrest will ease soon.

### 27. ★★★☆☆ Rosebank: Life on the oil field at the centre of a political storm

- **分类**: 🛢️ 能源
- **来源**: BBC Business / GN Energy / NPR World
- **多源确认**: 3
- **时间**: 2026-10-25T07:15:38+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Scotland editor James Cook finds out what life is like on board a ship in an untapped oil field awaiting its fate.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Scotland editor ,Â  Reporting from Rosebank oil field
When we landed by helicopter at Rosebank, this oil field was at the centre of a political storm - and set to be in the path of a real one.
We arrived on Wednesday morning, just a few hours after the UK government had granted consent for the production of gas from the Jackdaw field in the North Sea.
But already attention was shifting to this wild and windy patch of the North Atlantic â the next big decision for Energy Secretary Miatta Fahnbulleh.
Rosebank is bigger than Jackdaw, it would generate higher greenhouse gas emissions, and it could be far more lucrative for its owners, Adura and Ithaca.
Image caption, Nicola Boyd loves her job on the Petrojarl Rosebank vessel
This summer, along with a camera crew, I travelled to Jackdaw, 150 miles east of Aberdeen.
Now we were the first journalists to visit Rosebank.
We came here - days before Storm Boelo arrived with strong winds and heavy rain - to scrutinise a controversial project which is at the heart of a national debate about climate change, energy policy and the economy.
But we also hoped for a rare glimpse into life offshore.
"It&#x27;s good fun, it&#x27;s interesting, every day can change," said Nicola Boyd, who runs the helideck, and greeted us as we struggled out of our lifejackets and survival suits.
Boyd has worked offshore for nearly a decade, including a year on Rosebank - where she spends two weeks at a time followed by three weeks&#x27; leave.
Image caption, The Petrojarl Rosebank is moored in the North Atlantic waiting to see if production gets the go-ahead from the UK government
It&#x27;s also a job that calls for steady legs and a strong stomach.
Unlike the fixed Jackdaw platform, this installation â 80 miles north west of ShetlandÂ âÂ is a large ship, a floating production, storage and offloading vessel (FPSO) named the Petrojarl Rosebank.
It&#x27;s been chartered by the field&#x27;s owners from Aberdeen-based Altera and, during my 48 hours on board, it was constantly but gently rolling on the North Atlantic swell.
With the understatement typical of an Orkney islander, lead mechanical technician Ewan Leonard told me the winter weather could be "a bit of a challenge."
"Last week we had a 30m heave coming through," he said, referring to the swell, "so we&#x27;ll probably suffer more of that as the winter goes on."
Altera CEO Chris Brett told me that in the industry the environment west of Shetland is considered "as harsh as you get anywhere in the world."
Whatever your view on that industry, the sheer scale of what humans have achieved in such conditions is impressive.
Image caption, Ewan Leonard says the winter weather can be "a bit of a challenge"
The deep reserves of skill, ingenuity and engineering prowess among the 122 people on board are obvious.
The jobs are well-paid and, according to Ewan Leonard, the facilities are excellent.
"We have single-man cabins, there&#x27;s good gym facilities, there&#x27;s a lot of rec rooms here."
Douglas Hogge, a production supervisor in charge of a team responsible for the safe extraction of oil and gas, agrees.
"There&#x27;s the gym, the sauna," he said, adding "you can obviously go and get some fresh air. There&#x27;s plenty of fresh air out there."
Image caption, Food on board the ship was described as "fantastic"
"As you&#x27;ve seen yourself, the food is fantastic on board, so no complaints." added Leonard.
Everyone we spoke to agreed with that, praising the talented chefs who sent out all sorts of delicious dishes, from braised ox cheek to mackerel to pork loin, day and night.
As dusk gathers and the nightshift get ready for work with a strange breakfast of fillet steak and chocolate brownie pudding, the vessel begins to glow in the gloom.
It is a strange sight, the decks criss-crossed by a bewilderingly complex array of pipes, valves and bright yellow steelwork.
Image caption, The ship is an array of pipes, valves and bright yellow steelwork
The ship doesn&#x27;t move far, rotating around a fixed central point called the turret to face into the weather, making life a little easier for those on board.
If production is approved, oil and gas will be pumped up through the turret from wells in the field below, which contains an estimated 300m to 500m barrels, enough for perhaps 25 years.
"For myself and a lot of the guys, that would see us out to retirement," said Douglas Hogge.
Environmental campaigners strongly disagree. They&#x27;ve been trying to stop Rosebank for years, with some success.
"The future of the energy sector in the UK is renewable energy," said Tessa Khan, executive director of the campaigning group Uplift.
Consent for production at Rosebank was first granted under the Conservative government in 2023.
But in 2025, after a challenge by Greenpeace and Uplift, the Court of Session in Edinburgh ruled the approvals of the UK&#x27;s largest known untapped oil field and Jackdaw were unlawful because they had failed to take into account the climate impact of actually burning the fossil fuels in cars, homes and factories.
The judge, Lord Ericht, said the fields&#x27; developers could carry on with work short of producing oil and gas while they provided more detailed environmental assessments for the government and regulators to consider.
"We think Rosebank would be a disastrous decision and a terrible deal for ordinary people across this country," said Khan, urging prime minister Andy Burnham to prioritise renewables in his quest to re-industrialise the nation.
"If the government&#x27;s interested in building investor confidence, giving the green light to a major new oil field is going to do the opposite," she argued.
Whatever happens with Rosebank and Jackdaw, the long-term trend for the industry is clear and it is one of decline.
It&#x27;s a quarter of a century since oil production peaked and in recent years jobs in oil and gas have been disappearing much faster than they&#x27;re being created in renewable energy such as wind and solar power.
The oil from Rosebank would be loaded onto tankers via a gigantic hose and nozzle, which looks like the super-sized pump of an enormous floating filling station.
From there it would be sold on the international market and, most likely, taken to continental Europe for refining.
Opponents say that proves the project is of little benefit to the UK and should be rejected.
Supporters argue that&#x27;s not the case because UK and European energy markets are co-dependent, adding that Rosebank will generate millions in tax for the Treasury.
Neil McCulloch, chief executive of Adura â which owns 80% of Rosebank and is a joint venture between Shell and the Norwegian state energy firm, Equinor â is optimistic.
He points out that Rosebank contains not just oil but gas which would be piped to Shetland and on to the St Fergus terminal in Aberdeenshire, with the potential to heat 500,000 British homes at peak production.
Approval of this project, argues McCulloch, could pave the way for four more Jackdaw-sized gas developments in the waters west of Shetland â Tornado, Tornado East, Tobermory and Suilven.
A surge in fossil fuel production if Rosebank is approved is exactly what campaigners fear.
It would, argues Tessa Khan, exacerbate the deadly and damaging impact of climate change for British teachers, nurses, firefighters and families worried about their children while making "huge profits for companies that are already incredibly profitable".
Image caption, The oil from Rosebank would be loaded onto tankers via a gigantic hose and nozzle
When it comes to the impending UK government decision â which sources have told BBC News should come before Christmas â there is a strange mood at Rosebank.
The decision in favour of Jackdaw has clearly encouraged many on board but it has also highlighted the jeopardy they are facing.
For now, most workers we spoke to said they were just focusing on the job, with its strange rhythms and unusual restrictions â no ability to pop to the shops, no alcohol, a lot of special occasions missed.
For steward Liam Davidson, it is a desire to see his French bulldog, Bailey, at the end of his fortnight on board.
"My wee dog&#x27;s at the heliport when I arrive," he said, "I do miss her."
Image caption, It is hoped the decision on Rosebank will be made in the next few weeks
For Nicola Boyd, it&#x27;s her double bed and a pizza delivery.
"I love seeing the family, but for me, it&#x27;s the pizza," she told me.
But this time there might be a delay in delivering Boyd herself.
She is coming to the end of her fortnight but, as she helped us into our survival suits, the Met Office issued an amber weather warning for much of Scotland.
For Rosebank, it seems, there are stormy seas ahead, one way or the another.
What are you hoping for from the Budget? What questions do you have?
I will make final decision on Rosebank oil field, energy secretary insists
Burnham defends Jackdaw decision as Badenoch urges PM to overturn ban on new oil and gas licences

</details>

### 28. ★★★☆☆ 4 Investments That Could Make Sense If the Federal Reserve Raises Interest Rates - AOL.com

- **分类**: 💰 全球金融
- **来源**: GN Fed
- **多源确认**: 1
- **假设性事件**: 是（已降权）
- **时间**: 2026-10-25T11:30:10+00:00
- **发生了什么**: 4 Investments That Could Make Sense If the Federal Reserve Raises Interest Rates&nbsp;&nbsp;AOL.com

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

After the Federal Reserve recently decided to raise interest rates to cool an uptick in inflation, much of Wall Street and Main Street are trying to understand what its next move will be.
According to a recent CNBC article, the chances of a rate hike at the next Federal Reserve meeting in October have fallen below 20%, thanks to a weaker-than-expected September jobs report. However, this doesn’t mean the Federal Reserve won’t continue raising rates at future meetings if the economic environment changes.
Trending Now: Mark Cuban -- 6 Investments To Steer Clear of If You Like Your Money
Find Out: 8 Low-Effort Ways To Make Passive Income (You Can Start This Week)
Since several economists still believe additional rate hikes are possible, now is the time to start preparing your portfolio to take advantage. Here are a few investments to consider .
Floating-rate CLOs hold a number of corporate loans with floating-rate coupons. When the Fed Funds rate increases, these coupons are usually reset higher.
“The monthly coupons of these ETFs are generated by collateralized loan obligations whose interest is the sum of the SOFR (Secured Overnight Financing Rate) plus a margin,” said Ulrich Ebensperger, founder and CEO of Ziggma . “SOFR is roughly equal to the Fed Funds target rate give or take a few basis points. So not only do the monthly coupons rise with the Fed Rate increase, principal is also protected because of the floating rate nature of the bonds.”
Ebensperger recommended both JAAA and JBBB. JAAA will have lower credit risk because it offers AAA-rated CLO exposure. That lower risk will provide access to a 4.63% 30-day SEC yield. JBBB offers investors a higher 30-day SEC yield of 6.34%, but that comes with greater default risk . No matter the risk you want to take, these products will see higher yields if the Fed raises rates again.
Senior loan ETFs carry some investment risk because they’re made up of bonds from companies with below-investment-grade credit. However, with additional risk comes the potential for higher returns . Similar to floating-rate CLO ETFs, the coupon rate on senior loan ETFs will increase when the benchmark rate rises.
An example is the State Street Blackstone Senior Loan ETF ( SRLN ). It typically holds 80% of its assets in first-lien senior secured floating-rate bank loans, offering a 30-day SEC yield of 6.78%.
When interest rates rise, equity investors can consider bank stocks because banks’ net interest margin, the difference between interest earned on loans and interest paid to depositors, is also increasing.
“A Fed rate hike boosts banks&#x27; net interest margin,” Ebensperger said. “This difference typically increases as the Fed rate rises because banks pass on only a fraction of higher rates to depositors. The positive effect of higher rates on earnings is somewhat mitigated by higher default rates in the face of increased lending rates.”
Investing in bank stocks still carries risk. Even though banks are earning more from mortgages, consumer loans, commercial loans and credit lines, they’re also paying more for deposits, and there can be an increased number of defaults.
If you’re looking to minimize your risk, consider short-term Treasury bills, either held directly or through the iShares SGOV ETF . These have very short maturities, allowing investors to reinvest soon after interest rates rise.

### 31. ★★★☆☆ Iran Update, October 24, 2026 - Institute for the Study of War

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: GN Geopolitics
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-25T02:46:19+00:00

Weekday updates cover events from the past 24-hour period, whereas weekend updates are abbreviated and cover the Houthi-Saudi conflict and major breaking developments r

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

The Institute for the Study of War (ISW) and The Critical Threats Project (CTP) at the American Enterprise Institute are publishing updates Monday through Friday to provide analysis on the war with Iran and the broader Middle East. Weekday updates cover events from the past 24-hour period, whereas weekend updates are abbreviated and cover the Houthi-Saudi conflict and major breaking developments related to the war with Iran.
Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton warned on October 23 that US intelligence assessments indicate that Iran is in the "advanced stages" of planning attacks against US targets in the region.
Houthi supporters and military officers have reportedly expressed dissatisfaction with the group’s military progress near Taiz City and are urging Houthi leadership to conduct a large-scale ground assault to capture the city.
The Houthis claimed that they attacked three Saudi airports, pumping stations on the East-West Pipeline, and oil facilities at major Saudi Red Sea ports.
Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton warned on October 23 that US intelligence assessments indicate that Iran is in the "advanced stages" of planning attacks against US targets in the region. [1] Clayton noted in a memo addressed to Secretary of State Marco Rubio that Iran has “substantially increased” its efforts to target US personnel.[2] US officials told Western media that US intelligence agencies assess that the Iranian threat to US citizens in the Middle East is at the highest level since the US-Israel-Iran War began in February 2026.[3] Iran has repeatedly targeted US military and diplomatic personnel based in the region since the beginning of the war. Iran also reportedly planned a drone attack against Royal Air Force (RAF) Fairford in the United Kingdom, prompting the United States to evacuate 12 B-1 Lancers from the base in early October.[4] US officials told the New York Times on October 23 that the United States has started to reduce its diplomatic personnel in the region due to the threat from Iran.[5]
Clayton’s warning about Iranian plans to attack US targets comes amid recent Iranian threats to “expand” Iran’s target bank in the event that the United States or Israel resume strikes on Iran.[6] Iranian officials warned Arab officials and mediators that Iran would “more aggressively” target US forces and naval vessels in the event of renewed conflict.[7] These warnings are likely intended to deter any renewed US or Israeli military operations while Iran is simultaneously preparing for possible conflict. Iran reportedly conducted over 30 tests of medium-range solid-fuel Emad and Kheibar Shekan ballistic missiles on October 11, for example.[8]
Secure international recognition of Iranian control over the Strait of Hormuz
Degrade the US ability and willingness to continue the war
Restore deterrence vis- à -vis the United States and its regional partners
Divide the United States and Israel from the Arab states
Houthi supporters and military officers have reportedly expressed dissatisfaction with the group’s military progress near Taiz City and urged Houthi leadership to conduct a large-scale ground assault to capture the city. The Houthis have fully encircled Taiz City and surrounding areas to the south since October 12.[9] A Marib-based Yemeni journalist and a DC-based Yemen analyst posted social media messages from Houthi-aligned “activists” and military officers who argued that the Houthis’ ongoing siege of Taiz City is providing Yemeni government-aligned forces with excess time to consider options to free the city and exhaust Houthi forces in combat.[10] The messages from the military officers and Houthi supporters claimed that a protracted siege of Taiz City would be a "serious mistake" and bring about “catastrophic consequences” for the Houthis.[11] These messages and reports suggest that some Houthi-aligned figures are not confident in the group’s ability to compel government-aligned forces and civilians to surrender Taiz City, even though government-aligned forces defending the city are engaged in heavy combat and face significant supply shortages.[12]
Yemeni government-aligned forces’ defense of their positions on Taiz City’s western outskirts in recent weeks has also prevented the Houthis from quickly advancing and overwhelming government-aligned forces in the city.[13] Anti-Houthi Yemeni media reported on October 23 that Yemeni government-aligned forces recaptured the Hangar Checkpoint, which lies on a main roadway leading into Taiz City from the southwest, but ISW-CTP cannot verify this claim.[14] Saudi or Yemeni government-aligned forces also conducted at least four airstrikes targeting a major Houthi artillery battery at the abandoned Sofitel Hotel, which is located on a hilltop that overlooks Taiz City from the east.[15] The Houthis have used the hotel’s advantageous terrain to fire heavy artillery barrages on government-aligned forces’ positions in Taiz City since 2015.[16]
Yemeni government-aligned forces are continuing to engage Houthi fighters further south in Taiz and Lahij governorates in attempts to break the Houthis’ encirclement of Taiz City and central Taiz Governorate.[17] Anti-Houthi media posted a video of Yemeni government-aligned forces at a security force headquarters in al Misrakh district, central Taiz Governorate, on October 23 and claimed that government-aligned forces have recaptured the entire district.[18] ISW-CTP cannot confirm this claim, however. An independent Yemeni journalist reported on October 23 that Yemeni government-aligned forces engaged Houthi fighters near the southern edge of the Houthis’ encirclement in Taiz Governorate and along the Taiz-Aden ground line of communication (GLOC) in al Maqatirah district, Lahij Governorate, on October 23.[19] A breakout from the Houthis’ encirclement of central Taiz Governorate will provide little operational gain to Yemeni government-aligned forces unless these forces recapture, hold, and secure significant segments of the Taiz-Aden GLOC.[20]
The Houthis launched at least three ballistic missiles targeting the Yemeni Coast Guard’s administrative buildings in government-controlled Aden on October 24. The Yemeni government’s Interior Ministry confirmed that the Houthis struck a Coast Guard building at its headquarters, located near Aden International Airport, with two ballistic missiles.[21] Houthi military spokesperson Yayha Saree claimed that the Houthis struck the Yemeni government’s al Nasr Military Camp site, where the Yemeni Interior Ministry is now located.[22] The Yemeni government’s Interior Ministry also confirmed that a Houthi ballistic missile struck another Coast Guard building in Tawahi District.[23] Saree claimed that the Houthis also targeted the Naval Base in Aden, which is located in Tawahi District.[24] The Yemeni government’s Coast Guard is responsible for monitoring and intercepting Houthi maritime smuggling efforts.[25] The timing of Houthi strikes targeting government maritime assets is notable given that Saudi Arabia has increasingly struck Houthi smuggling vessels in the Red Sea since October 16.[26]
The Houthis have continued their attack campaign to impose economic and political costs on Saudi Arabia in order to compel Riyadh to capitulate to Houthi demands. The Saudi Joint Forces Command reported that Saudi air defenses intercepted a ballistic missile over Riyadh and a drone over eastern Saudi Arabia on October 23.[27] Saudi air defenses also intercepted ballistic missiles over Yanbu, Taif, Mecca, and Medina on October 24.[28] Houthi spokesperson Yahya Saree claimed that the Houthis fired ballistic missiles and drones on October 24 targeting King Khalid International Airport in Riyadh, King Fahd International Airport in Dammam, Najran International Airport, and King Khalid Air Base in Khamis Mushait, as well as a Saudi Aramco facility in Yanbu, a Petro Rabigh Refinery in Rabigh, and an unspecified number of pumping stations on the Saudi East-West pipeline.[29] The Houthis have repeatedly targeted Saudi critical infrastructure as part of the Houthis’ ongoing strike campaign to compel Saudi Arabia to halt military operations against the Houthis and accept the Houthis’ demands, which include lifting Saudi controls on Houthi-controlled ports and airports and granting the Houthis control over Yemen’s oil and gas resources.[30]

### 33. ★★★☆☆ Russian Offensive Campaign Assessment, October 24, 2026 - Institute for the Study of War

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: GN Geopolitics
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-25T02:46:19+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Russian Offensive Campaign Assessment, October 24, 2026&nbsp;&nbsp;Institute for the Study of War

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Russian Offensive Campaign Assessment, October 24, 2026
Ukrainian officials are confirming the resumption of unrestricted access to SpaceX’s low-earth orbit satellite communication technology (Starlink and Starshield) without disruptions in Ukraine on October 24. Ukrainian officials, including Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky, Ukrainian Defense Minister Yevheniy Khmara, and sources within the Ukrainian Ministry of Defense (MoD), reported on October 24 that Starlink/Starshield terminals are operating at speeds exceeding 161 kilometers per hour (about 100 miles per hour) and that Ukrainian forces are recording speeds of over 400 kilometers per hour (about 248 miles per hour) and reporting stable connections.[1]
Ukraine’s intermediate-range strike campaign against energy infrastructure in Russia and occupied Ukraine overnight on October 23 to 24 caused power outages across occupied Ukrainian oblasts including occupied Crimea. Krasnodar Krai Operational Headquarters claimed on October 24 that Ukrainian drone debris fell at a substation near Bely, Temryuk raion, Krasnodar Krai (about 69 kilometers from the Ukrainian border).[2] NASA Fire Information for Resource Management System (FIRMS) data from October 24 shows heat anomalies at the Taman electrical substation located near Bely, Krasnodar Krai that likely caused power outages in occupied Crimea and across occupied Ukraine, according to Ukrainian defense source Militarnyi.[3] The Taman substation is the main power node connecting all four lines of the Russian energy bridge that transmits electricity from Russia to occupied Crimea.[4] Data from the Ukrainian Unmanned Systems Forces (USF) between October 23 and 24 indicates that Ukrainian forces struck five “power hubs,” likely referring to electrical power stations, as part of Ukraine’s ongoing operation Crimea Switch Off in occupied Crimea.[5]
The Russian Ministry of Energy claimed on October 24 that there are power outages in occupied Crimea and occupied Kherson, Zaporizhia, Donetsk, and Luhansk oblasts due to an “external impact” on energy infrastructure facilities.[6] Zaporizhia Oblast occupation head Yevgeny Balitsky claimed on October 24 that a Ukrainian drone strike against an unspecified main power grid node outside of occupied Zaporizhia Oblast caused power outages in occupied Zaporizhia Oblast.[7] Sevastopol occupation governor Mikhail Razvozhaev claimed on October 24 that Ukrainian strikes against energy infrastructure outside of Sevastopol caused power outages in Sevastopol and added that public energy facilities are switching to backup power systems.[8] Razvozhaev noted that only 40 percent of Sevastopol apartment buildings have power and that Sevastopol is operating on a “two through six” schedule (residents receiving power for two hours and having outages for six hours).[9] Crimea occupation head Sergei Aksyonov claimed on October 24 that there are electricity and water supply restrictions in occupied Crimea following Ukrainian drone strikes and power outages outside of occupied Crimea.[10] A local Crimean-based Telegram channel claimed on October 23 that consumers observed simultaneous power outages in occupied Sevastopol, Simferopol, Yalta, Feodosia, Alushta, Dzhankoy, and other major settlements in occupied Crimea.[11] The channel noted that the power bridge into occupied Crimea is not working, and that occupied Crimea is relying on its own power generators.[12] Internet observation organization NetBlocks reported a sharp drop in internet traffic in occupied Crimea following Ukrainian drone strikes against energy infrastructure on October 24.[13]
Ukraine agreed to observe a limited ceasefire in Russian-occupied Oleshky, Kherson Oblast beginning on October 27, indicating a convergence between Russia and Ukraine on the ceasefire start date, but the geographical extent and other terms of the ceasefire remain unclear. The Ukrainian General Staff confirmed on October 23 that Ukraine will observe a ceasefire in a “designated area” of Russian-occupied Oleshky beginning at 0300 local Kyiv time on October 27 to conduct a humanitarian operation in occupied Oleshky.[14] Russian Chief of the General Staff Army General Valery Gerasimov stated on October 23 that Russia has agreed to the limited ceasefire “in the Kherson direction” beginning at 0400 local Moscow time (equivalent to 0300 Kyiv time) on October 27.[15] Russian Human Rights Commissioner Yana Lantratova claimed on October 23 that there will be a safe passage for citizens who wish to leave the combat zone.[16] Ukraine and Russia agreed to begin observing a ceasefire on the morning of October 27 but the precise geographical area that the ceasefire will cover and whether the Russian forces will provide a legitimate and duress-free choice to the Ukrainian Oleshky residents to evacuate to Ukraine or Russia remains unclear.
Ukrainian officials are confirming the resumption of unrestricted access to SpaceX’s low-earth orbit satellite communication technology (Starlink and Starshield) without disruptions in Ukraine on October 24.
Ukraine’s intermediate-range strike campaign against energy infrastructure in Russia and occupied Ukraine overnight on October 23 to 24 caused power outages across occupied Ukrainian oblasts including occupied Crimea.
Ukraine agreed to observe a limited ceasefire in Russian-occupied Oleshky, Kherson Oblast beginning on October 27, indicating a convergence between Russia and Ukraine on the ceasefire start date, but the geographical extent and other terms of the ceasefire remain unclear.
Russian forces recently advanced southeast of Dobropillya.
Ukrainian forces continued their long-range strike campaign against Russian military, energy, and industrial infrastructure on the night of October 23 to 24. Russian forces launched 126 drones against Ukraine overnight.
We do not report in detail on Russian war crimes because these activities are well-covered in Western media and do not directly affect the military operations we are assessing and forecasting. We will continue to evaluate and report on the effects of these criminal activities on the Ukrainian military and the Ukrainian population and specifically on combat in Ukrainian urban areas. We utterly condemn Russian violations of the laws of armed conflict and the Geneva Conventions and crimes against humanity even though we do not describe them in these reports.
Ukrainian forces continued their long-range strike campaign against Russian military, energy, and industrial infrastructure on the night of October 23 to 24. The Ukrainian General Staff reported that Ukrainian forces struck an unspecified Russian aircraft at Yeysk Airfield in Krasnodar Krai (roughly 178 kilometers from the frontline) and the Tolyattikauchuk petrochemical plant in Tolyatti, Samara Oblast (roughly 775 kilometers from the international border) on October 23 or overnight on October 23 to 24.[17] Geolocated footage published on October 24 shows a fire at the Tolyattikauchuk petrochemical plant following Ukrainian strikes.[18] Tolyatti City Mayor Ilya Sukhikh acknowledged that Ukrainian strikes damaged industrial facilities in the city.[19] A Ukrainian open-source intelligence (OSINT) channel posted footage on October 24 showing Ukrainian forces striking the Omskiy Kauchuk Chemical Plant and the Ozon warehouse in Omsk City, Omsk Oblast (roughly 2,500 kilometers from the international border), causing fires.[20] Geolocated footage published on October 24 shows the aftermath of the strike against the Omskiy Kauchuk Chemical Plant which reportedly damaged its D-3 and D-5 production units.[21] Omsk Oblast Governor Vitaliy Khotsenko acknowledged that Ukrainian forces struck Omsk City’s northern industrial zone.[22] Rostov Oblast Governor Yuri Slyusar reported on October 24 that Ukrainian drone debris fell on a Rostovpassazhirtrans trucking company branch in Rostov-on-Don, Rostov Oblast and damaged a transformer station in Kalinin, Rostov Oblast (both between roughly 190 and 200 kilometers from the frontline), causing localized power outages.[23]
The Ukrainian General Staff provided an updated battlefield damage assessment (BDA) of Ukraine's October 23 strike against the Proton-Electrotex plant in Oryol City, Oryol Oblast. The Ukrainian General Staff confirmed on October 24 that Ukraine’s October 23 strike damaged the main production building of the Proton-Electrotex plant — a manufacturer of optoelectronic equipment for S-71 Kover cruise missiles, Iskander-M ballistic missiles, and Pantsir-S1 air defense systems — in Oryol City, Oryol Oblast (roughly 161 kilometers from the international border).[24]
Russian objective: Create defensible buffer zones in Sumy Oblast along the international border
A Russian milblogger claimed on October 24 that Russian forces continued limited offensive operations in northern Sumy Oblast, but ISW did observe evidence that Russian forces advanced.[25] A source reporting on the Russian Northern Grouping of Forces claimed on October 24 that Russian forces are conducting a personnel rotation in Krasnopillya raion southeast of Sumy City.[26]
Russian objective: Push Ukrainian forces back from the international border to create a defensible buffer zone with Belgorod Oblast and approach Kharkiv City
Russian forces continued offensive operations in northern Kharkiv Oblast on October 23 and 24 but did not advance.[27] Ukrainian Joint Forces Group Spokesperson Colonel Viktor Trehubov stated on October 24 that Russian forces are actively attacking toward Vilcha, Vovchanski Khutory, and Bilyi Kolodyaz (all northeast of Kharkiv City).[28] Trehubov stated that terrain features in the area are forcing Russian forces to use the same routes repeatedly to attack the settlements, likely enhancing Ukrainian forces’ ability to detect and strike Russian infiltration groups.
Russian forces continued limited offensive operations in the Velykyi Burluk direction on October 23 and 24 but did not advance.[29]
Russian objective: Cross the Oskil River in Kharkiv Oblast and push westward into eastern Kharkiv Oblast and northern Donetsk Oblas t
Ukrainian drone and subsurface operations north of Kupyansk are denying Russian forces a staging ground from which to conduct infiltration missions into Kupyansk from the north. Ukrainian Joint Forces Group Spokesperson Colonel Viktor Trehubov stated on October 24 that Russian forces move through Holubivka (just north of Kupyansk) to reach Kupyansk but do not permanently station their troops in the settlement, indicating that Russian forces do not maintain a consistent presence in Holubivka.[30] Trehubov stated that Russian forces rarely manage to enter Radkivka (west of Holubivka and north of Kupyansk) and typically do so only during poor weather conditions, indicating that Ukrainian drone operations are preventing Russian forces from establishing control over Radkivka. A source reporting on the Russian Western Grouping of Forces claimed on October 23 that Ukrainian forces took control of a section of an underground pipeline extending from Radkivka to a point northeast of Holubivka where the pipeline passes beneath the Oskil River.[31] The source claimed that Ukrainian control of the pipeline in this area prevents Russian forces from reaching the northern part of Kupyansk. The source claimed that Ukrainian forces maintain drone air superiority in this area, which prevents Russian forces from using open terrain to conduct infiltration missions into Kupyansk.
A source reporting on the Russian Western Grouping of Forces claimed that Russian forces continued offensive operations northeast of Borova on October 24, but ISW has not observed evidence that Russian forces advanced.[32]
Russian Main Effort: Capture Ukraine's Fortress Belt and complete the seizure of Donetsk Oblast
Russian Subordinate Main Effort #1 – Encircle the Fortress Belt from the northwest
Russian objective: Seize Lyman and cross the Siverskyi Donets River to support a wide encirclement of the Fortress Belt – assessed objective of the Russian 25th Combined Arms Army and Western Grouping of Forces elements
Russian forces continued limited offensive operations in the Lyman direction on October 23 and 24 but did not advance.[33]
Russian Subordinate Main Effort #2 – Advance on the Fortress Belt from the east
Russian objective: Advance on Slovyansk, Kramatorsk, and Druzhkivka from the east – assessed objective of the Russian 3rd Combined Arms Army and Southern Grouping of Forces elements
Russian forces continued limited offensive operations east of Slovyansk on October 23 and 24 but did not advance.[34] Geolocated footage published on October 23 shows a Russian fiber-optic first-person view (FPV) drone striking a Ukrainian civilian evacuation vehicle in Slovyansk.[35]
Russian Subordinate Main Effort #3 – Advance on the Fortress Belt from the south
Russian objective: Advance on Kostyantynivka and Druzhkivka from the south and southwest, east of the Kazenyi Torets River – assessed objective of the Russian 8th Combined Arms Army and Southern Grouping of Forces elements
Russian forces continued offensive operations within, west of, and northwest of Kostyantynivka on October 23 and 24 but did not advance.[36]
Russian Subordinate Main Effort #4 – Encircle the Fortress Belt from the southwest
Russian objective: Conduct a wide encirclement of the Fortress Belt from near Dobropillya and Pokrovsk west of the Kazenyi Torets River – assessed objective of the Russian 51st Combined Arms Army and Central Grouping of Forces elements
Russian forces recently advanced southeast of Dobropillya. Ukrainian forces conducted an airstrike on a Russian position in central Bilytske (southeast of Dobropillya), according to geolocated footage published on October 23, indicating that Russian forces advanced in the area.[37]
Russian forces reportedly conducted a large motorized assault in the Pokrovsk direction on October 21. A Ukrainian brigade operating in the Pokrovsk direction reported on October 23 that Russian forces conducted the largest motorized assault in its area of responsibility (AoR) of 2026, using motorcycles and other vehicles.[38] The brigade added that Russian forces attempted to advance at dawn under low-visibility weather conditions, but that Ukrainian forces repelled the assault, eliminating 43 Russian personnel and injuring 12.
Russian Supporting Effort: Advance into Dnipropetrovsk Oblast
Russian objective: Interdict Ukrainian ground lines of communication that support Ukrainian positions in Donetsk Oblast and envelop Ukrainian positions in southwestern Donetsk Oblast – assessed objective of elements of the Russian Central and Eastern groupings of forces
Russian forces continued limited offensive operations in the Novopavlivka and Oleksandrivka directions on October 23 and 24 but did not advance.[39] A Russian milblogger claimed on October 24 that Ukrainian forces are counterattacking towards Perebudova (northeast of Oleksandrivka) along the left (western) bank of the Mokri Yaly River.[40]
A Russian milblogger claimed on October 24 that Russian forces advanced north of Marivka (northeast of Novopavlivka).[41]
Russian objective: Maintain frontline positions, secure rear areas against Ukrainian strikes, and advance within tube artillery range of Zaporizhzhia City – assessed objective of the Russian Eastern and Dnepr Grouping of Forces
Russian forces continued offensive operations in the Hulyaipole direction on October 23 and 24 but did not advance.[42] Russian milbloggers claimed on October 24 that Russian forces advanced toward Novosoloshyne (northwest of Hulyaipole and 12 kilometers from the furthest Russian advances).[43] The milbloggers’ reporting patterns are consistent with the Kremlin’s systematic cognitive warfare effort to fabricate claims of Russian tactical successes in areas where Russian forces do not maintain enduring positions to falsely portray the frontline as collapsing, contrary to all available evidence.[44]
Russian forces continued limited offensive operations in the Orikhiv direction on October 23 and 24 but did not advance, as Ukrainian forces counterattacked.[45]
Neither Russian nor Ukrainian sources reported ground activity in the Kherson direction on October 24.
Russian forces continue to hunt civilians using human safari tactics in Kherson Oblast. Footage published on October 24 shows a Russian drone striking a civilian walking a dog in Kherson Oblast.[46] Russian forces in the Kherson direction have long employed human safari tactics, in which Russian drone operators purposefully hunt civilians as part of the broader effort to institutionalize intentional civilian harm as a tool of war.[47]
See topline text for reporting on power outages in occupied Crimea.
Russian Objective: Target Ukrainian military and civilian infrastructure in the rear and on the frontline
Note: The Ukrainian military ceased regularly reporting the number of Russian missiles fired and the number of missiles intercepted as of August 11.[48]
Russian forces conducted a series of drone strikes against Ukraine overnight on October 23 to 24. The Ukrainian Air Force reported that Russian forces launched 126 Shahed-type (61 of which were jet-powered) and Gerbera-type strike drones, Banderol and Dan-T loitering munitions, and Parodiya-type decoy drones from the direction of Shatalovo, Smolensk Oblast; Oryol, Bryansk, and Kursk Cities; Primorsko-Akhtarsk, Krasnodar Krai; occupied Donetsk City; and occupied Hvardiiske, Crimea.[49] The Ukrainian Air Force reported that Ukrainian forces downed 101 drones and loitering munitions, that an unspecified number of aerial vehicles struck 14 locations, and debris fell at five locations.
Russian forces continue striking Ukrainian bridges across the Dnipro River as part of their campaign to degrade Ukraine’s economy and logistics across the Dnipro River. Geolocated footage published on October 24 shows Russian strikes against the Zaporizhzhia Cable-Stayed Bridge in Zaporizhzhia City.[50]
Russian forces fielded two new small cruise missiles based on the Geran-5 drone as part of Russia’s continued focus on producing scalable and more cost-effective ground- and air-launched cruise missiles and jet-powered drones that are difficult for Ukrainian forces to intercept at scale. Ukrainian defense source Militarnyi reported on October 23 that Russian forces have introduced two modified Geran-5 K5-K variants that feature redesigned fuselages with changes to their tail assembly, wing design, and internal turbojet engine mounting.[51] Ukrainian military personnel stated to Militarnyi that Russian designers made changes to the design to increase the missiles’ maximum speed to 900 kilometers per hour, comparable to the Russian Kh-101 cruise missile.
Russian efforts to increase its military presence in Belarus and further integrate Belarus into Russian-favorable frameworks

<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Embassies on alert as Iran ramps up threats, U.S. elections loom
U.S. embassy officials in the Middle East were on high alert as new reports said Iran is in "advanced stages" of planning attacks against Americans.
The alerts followed the U.S. order for non-emergency government employees to leave Saudi Arabia this week.
The Trump administration has vowed renewed military strikes on Iran after the U.S. midterm elections on Nov. 3.
The U.S. embassy HQ in Riyadh is pictured on March 3, 2026.
U.S. embassy officials were on heightened alert around the Middle East as new reports said Iran is in the "advanced stages" of planning attacks against Americans Friday.
A memo from Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton said agencies received intelligence that Iran has "substantially increased efforts to target U.S. personnel" in the region, according to a New York Times report , and that the threats applied to "certain other countries" as well.
The warnings come after the U.S. ordered non-emergency government employees to leave Saudi Arabia this week, as attacks from the Iran-backed Houthis have worsened and the broader war between the U.S. and Tehran nears its eighth month.
The Saudi-led coalition fighting the Houthis said it destroyed two ballistic missiles fired by the group, aimed towards Taif and Yanbu.
Despite rising tensions, an Iranian official told The Associated Press on Saturday that Tehran remains open to talks with the U.S. under the former Pakistan-brokered deal that was agreed to in June.
Reza Amiri Moghadam, Iran&#x27;s ambassador to Islamabad, said Iranian officials are prepared to reopen the Strait of Hormuz if the Trump administration lifts its naval blockade, AP reported, but he said Washington has rebuffed the proposal. Later in the day, Iranian state media denied the report.
The Trump administration has vowed to put off military strikes on Iran after the midterm elections on Nov. 3, as the fallout from the war in Iran has caused a major disruption in the U.S. economy — with inflation and affordability the top concerns for voters who will decide control of Congress early next month.
Rising Middle East tensions sent oil prices soaring this week. Benchmark Brent crude futures topped $116 a barrel, a six-month high . U.S. West Texas Intermediate rose 3.8% to settle over $103 a barrel.

### 35. ★★★☆☆ Britain must be 'ready for war' by 2030 amid Russian aggression, UK defense secretary says - The Jerusalem Post

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: GN Geopolitics
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-25T07:31:37+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Britain must be 'ready for war' by 2030 amid Russian aggression, UK defense secretary says&nbsp;&nbsp;The Jerusalem Post

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Britain must be &#x27;ready for war&#x27; by 2030 amid Russian aggression, UK defense secretary says
Former UK army chief Gen. Sir Patrick Sanders also commented on the threat facing Britain, telling The Telegraph that he doesn&#x27;t know "what more signals we need."
Britain&#x27;s Defense Minister Wes Streeting speaks during a meeting with US Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth at the Pentagon in Washington, DC, on October 15, 2026.
( photo credit : Nathan Howard / POOL / AFP via Getty Images )
UK Defense Secretary Wes Streeting warned that the country must prepare for war as Russia's hybrid warfare against NATO intensifies, writing his comments in the London-based newspaper The Telegraph  on Saturday.
“My defining priority... is to make sure that this country is ready to fight and win a war," wrote Streeting. “To prevent the worst, we have to prepare for it. In the first instance, that means expanding our capabilities so our adversaries think twice about attacking us. Should that fail, we must ensure that Britain is ready for war.”
Streeting wrote that the United Kingdom must “be ready with our allies to fight and win,” warning that the country could face enemies able to attack it as early as 2030.
“Our adversaries’ willingness to harm us is not new," Streeting continued. "What has changed is their capacity. By 2030, their force strength and war economy could put them in a position to attack. That gives us a little over 1,000 days to complete our mission: be ready, with our allies, to fight and win."
He added that the UK government "will make sure it is mission accomplished.”
RAF Typhoon Fighter Jets operate from RAF Coningsby on October 21, 2026 in Coningsby, England. (credit: Ryan Jenkinson/Getty Images)
Streeting's warning follows the start of the UK's “Be Ready” campaign, which aims to prepare the country's citizens and residents for war by stocking at least three days of essential supplies ahead of time, according to The Telegraph .
Turning to debates in the UK regarding defense spending, Streeting wrote that he is "certainly willing to have them," but that “the Conservatives must face up to the fact that they left Britain with the smallest army since the Napoleonic Wars, missed every single recruitment target and oversaw plunging morale.”
“This Government is already delivering the biggest sustained increase in defense spending since the end of the Cold War. I am 100 percent confident that under this Labour prime minister and chancellor we will meet our NATO spending target,” he wrote. “The public want to see us doing more and investing more. That comes with choices and trade-offs for government spending in other areas, so I feel sharply the responsibility on my department to use every penny wisely.”
World at least as dangerous as Cold War, former UK army chief says
Former UK army chief Gen. Sir Patrick Sanders also commented on the threat facing Britain, telling The Telegraph that he doesn't know "what more signals we need for us to realize that if we don’t act now and we don’t act in the next five years to increase our resilience."
“The world has become as dangerous, if not more dangerous, than it was in the Cold War , and so I always worry about the layers of security we have, but I worry more about our resilience, our ability to recover from things, from strikes, to protect our civilian population, to make sure the civilian population understands that these are no longer things that happen elsewhere; they could happen in the UK,” said Sanders.

</details>

### 36. ★★★☆☆ Iran says won’t allow US warships to get close as Trump ponders new attacks - Al Jazeera

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: GN Geopolitics
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-24T13:15:07+00:00
- **发生了什么**: <a href="https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMitwFBVV95cUxPbnhYekZtTGYtNUxRY1l0SkNlclVxV1JhVHJ2QUNzRFhRQW96Z0FWbXBSakNhblFEYk9kNHBibExfT0xFVFhLbUEzLWZpR0VNVC0tT2NlTTRIWmlaRThSTXN5OEF6d1ZiSEhGak11ck5zclJpYkRPODdJdFNqLXJTWjZiemlRSnZuOUV1MWY3eDZYMTBjaThzRDJaU3VVQnFqbFQ3bEt2enJEeHNUcm1nZTBnT1lXNXfSAbwBQVVfeXFMTzcyR081VVdqUVNRX05XQ0NxRFFPdm81M0NEV0hPcm9FYlZmTEJ4bTBPQUtjSjZvOEx3aGFWdGNDbHkyZzdoVDlVZzZyU1NrNkt4YkhBZGxZcm1vZ0tlWTB0T3lZbDdaX3dnSkZVdUpqc19KZnpWS3Vlc2tiN1JEQUNWZEVZYmV0ZWVyYXFENDU4a2FqU3

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Share Iran says won’t allow US warships to get close as Trump ponders new attacks on social media
A billboard depicting Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu in the empty hall of the United Nations, on a building in Tehran, Iran, October 13, 2026 [Majid Asgaripour/WANA via Reuters]
Tehran, Iran – Iran’s armed forces say they are prepared to fire on United States warships as President Donald Trump said Washington was still deliberating new attacks or continued pressure on Iran.
Trump told supporters at a rally in Florida on Friday that the US military “may do another Epic Fury”, in reference to the extensive bombing campaign against Iran at the start of the war. He had earlier questioned whether to end the conflict quickly through huge attacks on Iran or let pressure on Tehran from a naval blockade and sanctions persist.
list of 3 items list 1 of 3 Lebanese sceptical Israeli polls will bring change, end occupation of south
list 2 of 3 Keeping the world safe when no one is in charge
list 3 of 3 Why is Israel launching a new military operation in the occupied West Bank?
But Ali Abdollahi, chief of general staff of the Iranian armed forces, asserted that the US Navy is incapable of re-entering Iran’s territorial waters or its periphery due to Iran’s ability to target any vessel in the region.
“The US president can only dream of their soldiers and military again entering the Persian Gulf. They will take this wish to the grave for good,” he said in a statement carried by state media on Saturday morning.
Abdollahi also said US rhetoric about the war pointed to a basic contradiction: US claims that Iran’s military capabilities have been mostly destroyed during the war were nullified by its refusal to bring its aircraft carriers and support vessels closer to Iranian territory.
He added that Iranian forces were determined to “drown” the US and Israel in a “quagmire” in retaliation for their surprise attack on Iran on February 28, starting an eight-month war and plunging the whole region into instability.
Vessels in and around the Strait of Hormuz continue to be attacked by Iran. Together with the increased odds of renewed war, this has driven up oil prices and sent Brent crude to its highest price since April, at about $117.
The Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC) said seven tankers were attacked or hit mines overnight into Thursday. It then claimed on Friday that two supertankers struck mines. The IRGC has said its military doctrine is now more offensive, and Washington has reportedly ordered many diplomatic personnel to leave Saudi Arabia, with others set to depart other countries in the region .
The Iran-allied Houthis in Yemen also continued to exchange attacks with Saudi Arabia, hitting Riyadh again on Saturday, with the international airport in Dammam and the Shaybah oilfield targeted in recent days. Saudi forces have bombed Marib, Taiz and several other Yemeni provinces under Houthi control.
In southern Lebanon, Friday brought further reported Israeli air attacks around Nabatieh and artillery fire near Zawtar ash-Sharqiyah. Iran has linked this front to its war with the US and Israel, in the past demanding that any ceasefire with Washington also cover Lebanon .
Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu, facing an election on October 27 which comes days before the November 3 mid-term elections in the US, also ordered a lockdown of the occupied West Bank after a Palestinian attack killed two soldiers in Hebron. The Houthis have also launched attacks on Israel linked to events in Gaza and the West Bank, highlighting how wider regional peace now depends on events in multiple theatres, including Lebanon, the occupied West Bank and Gaza.
Netanyahu has reiterated his desire to overthrow the Iranian government, leading several state-linked media and analysts in Iran to claim that Tehran has activated a “dead hand” doctrine. That allegedly enables military attacks against US, Israeli and regional interests to continue from across the country’s 31 provinces if senior Iranian figures are again assassinated as at the start of the war .
The IRGC has threatened seabed internet and communication cables if Iran’s civilian infrastructure is again targeted by the US and Israel. US-based Axios on Saturday cited US intelligence claiming that Iranian authorities had “substantially increased” efforts to strike American officials in the Middle East and beyond.
Mediated negotiations between Iran and the US are no closer to yielding results. But regional and European powers held a coordination meeting in Istanbul on Friday and announced their expectations for the future of the war.
Top diplomats from Turkiye, Saudi Arabia, Pakistan and Egypt met with the United Kingdom, France, Germany and Italy, with neither the US nor Iran participating.
“We agree that a new attack on Iran would widen the war and spread the conflict across the region,” Turkiye’s Foreign Minister Hakan Fidan said after the meeting. “Military intervention in Iran is by no means a solution. No one is endorsing it at this point.”
German Foreign Minister Johann Wadephul said his government held Iran and the Houthis responsible for instability in the Middle East.
But the foreign ministers of the four regional countries issued a joint statement that said “Israeli occupation was the main source of instability in the region and beyond.”
US Navy sailors work on the flight deck of the aircraft carrier USS George Washington as it transits the Arabian Sea to support US military operations in the war with Iran on August 20, 2026 [US Navy/Handout via Reuters] In line with resolving the US-Israel war on Iran, the four regional countries supported the Islamabad memorandum of understanding signed in June and a negotiated settlement, while demanding unrestricted passage through the Strait of Hormuz . The group said they opposed attacks on civilians and commercial vessels, and condemned Houthi attacks on Saudi Arabia and its Red Sea shipping interests.
The Istanbul meeting once again displayed a “gap between diplomatic claims and realities in the field”, Nour News, an outlet affiliated with Iran’s Supreme National Security Council, argued in an analysis on Saturday.
It said the US and European powers – not Iran – must make concessions first. The outlet presented reopening the Strait of Hormuz as conditional on the US accepting Tehran’s demands and acknowledging responsibility for attacking Iran and Yemen.
Most Iranian newspapers on Saturday focused on signs of war, including rising oil prices.
The headline from hardline Vatan-e Emrooz reported that the price of a barrel of Brent crude rising above $117 was “evidence of disgrace for Trump”. Reformist Etemad said US and Israeli actions were signalling that the “zero hour” of another intense round of the war was approaching.
Reza Nasri, a Tehran-based foreign policy analyst with links to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, said that after being subject to decades of attacks, nuclear sabotage, sanctions and violated deals by the US, Iran’s strategy was now “enough is enough.
“Iran has reached its verdict. It will no longer negotiate under bombardment, honour agreements the other side treats as optional, or accept the killing of its children and the penury of its people as routine instruments of policy,” he wrote on X. “It will put an end to this cycle, and it will do so by all necessary means”.

</details>

Sunray warns of stronger-than-normal gas odor after over-odorization
City limit sign for Sunray, Texas (Credit: Google Maps)
SUNRAY, Texas (KVII) — The City of Sunray has over-odorized its natural gas system, which could cause a stronger-than-normal gas smell in the area, the Sunray Volunteer Fire Department said.
The department urged residents to use caution and not assume a strong odor is harmless because of the over-odorization.
Natural gas is odorless in its natural state. Utilities add an odorant to help people detect gas leaks . The odorant smells similar to rotten eggs.
If you smell gas indoors, leave the building and move to a safe location. Do not operate electrical switches, use open flames or do anything that could create a spark. The fire department also advised residents to open windows and call 911 from a safe location if the odor persists or they have concerns.
